# 150 Winter Nature-Fail Escape Prompts — Seedance 2.0 (15s, 9:16)

Every prompt: exactly 15 seconds, USA/Canada snow-and-ice locations, a calm natural first second followed by a sudden nature failure, and an escape made possible by an animal or a nearby person. No professional rescues. Each prompt sits in its own code block — use the copy button on the block.

## Prompt 001 — Backyard Pond Rink Splits Under the Spin — Duluth, Minnesota

```text
1. FORMAT
Vertical 9:16 smartphone video, 1080x1920, 60fps, exactly 15.0 seconds, one continuous real-time take with no cuts, no slow motion, no speed ramps and no transitions. Native in-camera audio only — no music, no voice-over, no captions, no on-screen text, no logos, no watermark. It must read as a real clip filmed by an ordinary person in the moment and posted unedited.

2. LOOK
Late afternoon in mid-December, -12°C, low winter sun skimming orange across the ice, long blue shadows from the birches, the rink surface scuffed with skate marks and patches of clear black ice near the far edge where a spring feeds the pond. Looks exactly like an unedited clip straight off a modern iPhone or Pixel: auto white balance drifting slightly cooler in shade and warmer near artificial light, highlight roll-off on sunlit snow, honest contrast, occasional lens flare only when a real light source enters frame, natural softness at the extreme edges of the lens. Skin shows real texture, redness from the cold and dry, chapped lips. Nothing stylized, nothing graded, nothing glossy.

3. CAMERA
Greg films handheld from the snowbank at the rink's edge, phone at chest height, framing Lily in full body with Moose sitting on the snowbank in the lower left. Real person filming on a phone: the frame breathes and bobs with the operator's body, the grip shifts when they gasp, focus briefly pulses before locking onto the nearest face or animal, and the auto-exposure visibly brightens or darkens as the scene changes. During the crisis the framing becomes messy and partly misses the action for a moment before the operator recovers it. No cinematic dolly moves, no perfect composition, no impossible angles.

4. LOCATION
A homemade backyard rink cleared on a small frozen pond behind a blue two-story clapboard house in the Lakeside neighborhood of Duluth, Minnesota. Snowbanks shoveled into a ring around the ice, a plastic hockey net, a red snow shovel stuck upright, bare birch trees and Lake Superior's grey horizon visible between houses.

5. CHARACTERS
Lily Hartmann, 10, slim, freckled, strawberry-blonde braid under a lavender knit pom-pom hat, white figure skates, black leggings, a puffy teal jacket; her father Greg Hartmann, 41, stocky, short brown beard, grey Carhartt beanie, tan canvas work jacket, jeans, felt-lined Sorel boots, filming. Moose, the family's 4-year-old Bernese Mountain Dog — large, tricolor black coat with rust eyebrows and cheeks, white blaze and chest, thick fluffy tail, frost on his whiskers, a worn red nylon collar with a brass tag. Character consistency is locked: faces, body proportions, hairstyles, clothing colors, accessories and the animal's exact markings stay identical from the first frame to the last — no outfit changes, no face drift, no breed or coat changes.

6. HOOK (00:00–00:02)
00:00–00:01: Calm and ordinary — Lily glides backward and starts a proud little spin as Greg says "Okay, show me the spin." 00:01–00:02: Mid-spin a sharp gunshot-loud CRACK rings across the pond, a white star fracture explodes outward from under her blade and dark water instantly wells up through the lines.

7. TIMELINE
00:00–00:01 — Lily skates backward smiling, arms out, blade hissing on the ice; Moose sits upright on the snowbank, ears relaxed.
00:01–00:02 — She begins a spin; a loud crack echoes, a white fracture star bursts under her skates and black water bubbles up through the seams.
00:02–00:03 — Lily freezes with arms locked out, the ice sags a few centimeters with a groaning creak; Greg gasps "Lily, don't move!"
00:03–00:04 — Moose launches off the snowbank before Greg can move, belly-low, paws splayed wide on the solid ice near the edge, tail straight out.
00:04–00:05 — The slab beneath Lily tilts; water sloshes over her blades; she shrieks and drops to her knees as Greg yells "Lie flat, flat!"
00:05–00:06 — Lily flattens out on her stomach; Moose stops a body length away on still-white ice, whining, front legs braced, and stretches his neck forward.
00:06–00:07 — Lily grabs the thick fur and red collar at Moose's neck with both mittens; the dog immediately backs up hard, haunches dropping, claws scraping.
00:07–00:08 — The slab behind her breaks free and rolls into the water with a heavy slosh as her legs slide off it onto the stronger ice.
00:08–00:09 — Moose keeps backpedaling, dragging Lily on her belly across the ice, leaving a wet streak; Greg drops to his knees at the edge, phone shaking.
00:09–00:10 — Greg grabs Lily's jacket hood with one hand and hauls her over the lip of the snowbank; the camera tilts wildly, showing sky and birch branches.
00:10–00:11 — Frame recovers: Lily sits in the snow sobbing, leggings soaked dark to the knee; Moose shoves his head into her chest, tail sweeping snow.
00:11–00:12 — Behind them a jagged black hole the size of a kitchen table steams faintly; broken ice plates bob and knock together.
00:12–00:13 — Greg pulls Lily close: "You're okay, you're okay, I got you." His breath fogs over the lens briefly.
00:13–00:14 — Lily, crying and laughing, wraps both arms around Moose's neck; Moose licks the tears and frost off her cheek.
00:14–00:15 — Greg points the camera back at the hole, exhales "Spring-fed. Never again," and the clip ends on Moose standing guard between the girl and the water.

8. SOUND DESIGN
Skate blades hissing and carving, a sharp rifle-like crack with a long pinging echo across the frozen pond, deep groaning ice, water sloshing and gurgling up through fractures, the dog's claws scraping and his urgent whine, Lily's scream and sobs, Greg's breathless shouts, snowbank crunch, a distant pickup truck passing on the street. Every sound is diegetic and captured by the phone's built-in microphone from the filming position. Absolutely no music, no score, no risers, no whooshes, no cinematic bass hits.

9. AUDIO REALISM
Recorded as one live phone mic track: ambient cold-air quietness first (which makes the sudden event hit harder), then a real transient spike, mic overload crackle, and the room or landscape ringing out afterwards. Off-axis sounds are duller, close sounds are sharper. Exhales and gasps are audible, the animal's panting, barking, snorting or calls are species-true, and every footstep or impact lines up with the picture to the frame. Speech is unscripted, imperfect, with stumbles, swearing replaced by real-sounding exclamations, and natural accents for the region.

10. 4K/8K VISUAL QUALITY ENHANCEMENT
Even if the render is 1080p, it must deliver a premium 4K/8K-grade feel: razor-sharp micro-detail in the Bernese's long silky tricolor fur with frost clinging to the guard hairs, the white star fracture lines inside clear black ice, wet dark leggings fabric and individual skate-scratch grooves; individual snowflakes, ice crystals and spindrift resolved in the air; realistic skin with pores, fine facial hair, cold-reddened cheeks, chapped lips and visible breath vapor; true fabric weave on knit hats, nylon shells, fleece and denim; accurate contact shadows under boots, paws and hooves; natural depth with gentle phone-lens background falloff; clean edges with no oversharpening halos, no smearing, no waxy smoothing and no noise-reduction mush. Lighting is physically motivated by the real sky, sun and practical light sources only, with correct specular highlights on ice and wet surfaces. Targeting Seedance 2.5-level realism through Seedance 2.0.

11. REALISM AND CONTINUITY
Lily keeps her lavender pom-pom hat and teal jacket on the whole time and her leggings stay wet after she exits; Moose's red collar and brass tag stay visible; the hole in the ice stays the same shape once it forms. Physics must be correct: snow and ice crack, slide and fall with real weight, momentum and debris that scatters and settles; near-freezing water looks dark, heavy and slushy; nothing floats, morphs or reverses. Human anatomy is correct at all times — five fingers per hand, natural joint limits, believable balance, real slips, stumbles and recoveries. Animal behavior is species-accurate in gait, ear and tail signals, vocalizations and reactions; the animal never acts like a trained movie actor or a cartoon. Footprints, paw prints and disturbed snow persist once made. Breath vapor appears with exhales in the cold. Time of day, light direction and weather stay consistent for all 15 seconds.

12. AVOID
Plastic or waxy skin, airbrushed faces, doll eyes, animated or rubbery bodies, extra or fused fingers, warped limbs, morphing faces, changing outfits, shifting animal markings, CGI-looking snow, fake particle snow, glossy video-game ice, unnatural slow motion, cinematic music, title text, subtitles, watermarks, logos, lens dirt overlays, film grain filters, teal-orange grading, over-saturated colors, drone shots, impossible camera moves, perfect studio lighting, humans or animals floating, objects passing through each other, physics that reverses, professional rescue crews, uniforms, sirens, helicopters, staged hero poses, gore, blood or graphic injury. Also avoid: the dog walking on water, ice behaving like glass shards, the girl being lifted dry out of water, a perfectly round CGI hole in the ice.
```

## Prompt 002 — Roof Avalanche Explodes Through the Picture Window — Fairbanks, Alaska

```text
1. FORMAT
Vertical 9:16 smartphone video, 1080x1920, 30fps, exactly 15.0 seconds, one continuous real-time take with no cuts, no slow motion, no speed ramps and no transitions. Native in-camera audio only — no music, no voice-over, no captions, no on-screen text, no logos, no watermark. It must read as a real clip filmed by an ordinary person in the moment and posted unedited.

2. LOOK
Midday in January but the sun barely clears the hills — dim pinkish daylight outside, -31°C, ice fog in the trees, warm amber lamplight and stove glow inside, frost ferns creeping up the corners of the window. Documentary-real phone footage: exposure set by the phone's auto mode so snow sits just below clipping and shadows hold a little noise; colors are muted and natural, never saturated; breath vapor catches the light; snow surfaces show wind ripples, sastrugi, boot holes and blue shadow. No color grade, no filters, no artificial glow, no dreamy haze, no sharpening halos.

3. CAMERA
Dana films handheld from the kitchen doorway about four meters away, catching Noah at the window, the stove on the right, and Kobuk lying on the rug. Imperfect amateur camerawork that sells authenticity: slight tilt, uneven headroom, quick reframing, a moment of the camera pointing at the snow or sky while the operator moves, then snapping back to the subject. Autofocus locks on the closest high-contrast object and occasionally racks between foreground snowflakes and the subject. Motion blur appears naturally on fast movement at a 1/60–1/120 phone shutter.

4. LOCATION
The living room of a log home on the outskirts of Fairbanks, Alaska: honey-colored log walls, a cast-iron wood stove glowing, a braided rug, a worn leather couch, a large single-pane picture window looking onto a snow-buried yard and black spruce, the eave of a steep metal roof just above the glass outside.

5. CHARACTERS
Noah Peterson, 7, round-faced, dark-brown mop of hair, wearing a grey thermal top with a moose print, red flannel pajama pants and wool socks; his mother Dana Peterson, 36, Alaska Native (Athabascan) features, long black hair in a low ponytail, oversized green fleece, filming from the kitchen doorway. Kobuk, a 6-year-old Alaskan Malamute — heavy-boned, wolf-grey and white coat with a classic white face mask and dark cap, plume tail curled over his back, amber-brown eyes, no collar indoors. Character consistency is locked: faces, body proportions, hairstyles, clothing colors, accessories and the animal's exact markings stay identical from the first frame to the last — no outfit changes, no face drift, no breed or coat changes.

6. HOOK (00:00–00:02)
00:00–00:01: Peaceful — Noah presses his nose and both palms to the frosty window, drawing in the frost with a finger while the stove crackles. 00:01–00:02: Kobuk suddenly jerks his head up, ears snapping toward the ceiling, and lets out a deep alarmed woo-woo howl as a heavy rumble rolls across the metal roof overhead.

7. TIMELINE
00:00–00:01 — Noah draws a smiley face in the window frost, giggling; Kobuk lies on the rug, head on paws, the stove ticking.
00:01–00:02 — Kobuk's head snaps up, ears forward, eyes on the ceiling; he gives a deep, rising howl as a low rumble starts sliding across the roof.
00:02–00:03 — The rumble grows into a freight-train roar overhead; Dana's voice: "What is that?" Kobuk is already up and lunging toward the window.
00:03–00:04 — The 45-kilo dog slams his shoulder into Noah's side, knocking the boy sideways off his feet onto the couch cushions.
00:04–00:05 — A wall of snow and roof ice crashes past the window outside and the big pane bursts inward in a white explosion of glass and powder.
00:05–00:06 — Snow chunks and glass fragments blast across the exact spot where Noah stood, burying the rug; the stove hisses as powder hits it.
00:06–00:07 — Dana screams "NOAH!" and the camera lurches forward, the frame swinging across log walls and the cloud of swirling snow.
00:07–00:08 — The powder settles: Noah is on the couch, wide-eyed, pajamas dusted white but unhurt; Kobuk stands over him, shaking snow off his coat.
00:08–00:09 — Frigid air pours in, a sheet of ice fog rolling across the floor; the curtains whip and flap violently.
00:09–00:10 — Dana grabs Noah under the arms and pulls him into the kitchen doorway; the camera points at the floor, showing glass and snow on the boards.
00:10–00:11 — Frame rises: a waist-high drift of snow and ice slabs fills the window area, bits of window frame hanging, black spruce visible outside.
00:11–00:12 — Kobuk trots to the boy and leans his whole weight against him, tail low and wagging, breathing in big puffs of vapor.
00:12–00:13 — Noah, trembling, hugs the dog's thick neck: "Kobuk pushed me." His voice cracks.
00:13–00:14 — Dana, out of breath, pans back to the destroyed window and the snow still trickling off the eave: "That was three feet of roof snow."
00:14–00:15 — She kneels and pulls both boy and dog into her arms; the clip ends on Kobuk's frosted muzzle pressed into Noah's shoulder.

8. SOUND DESIGN
Wood stove crackle and tick, a soft furnace hum, the dog's deep malamute "woo" howl, a rising sliding rumble on the metal roof turning into a roar, the explosive shatter of a large pane of glass, heavy snow thumping onto floorboards, a sizzle as snow hits the hot stove, roaring cold wind and flapping curtains, Dana's scream, the boy's shaky voice. Every sound is diegetic and captured by the phone's built-in microphone from the filming position. Absolutely no music, no score, no risers, no whooshes, no cinematic bass hits.

9. AUDIO REALISM
Authentic sound capture: the stillness of a snowy place (soft, dead acoustics because snow absorbs sound), distant natural background noise appropriate to the location, then sudden loud real-world sounds with proper attack and decay. The phone's noise suppression subtly pumps after loud sounds. People speak in quick fragments, breathing hard; the animal's sounds are recorded close and raw. No sweeteners, no Foley that does not match the action, no reverb that does not match the space.

10. 4K/8K VISUAL QUALITY ENHANCEMENT
Even if the render is 1080p, it must deliver a premium 4K/8K-grade feel: razor-sharp micro-detail in the Malamute's coarse wolf-grey guard hairs and woolly undercoat, frost-fern patterns on the glass, fractured glass edges, cold powder melting on hot cast iron, log grain and checks; individual snowflakes, ice crystals and spindrift resolved in the air; realistic skin with pores, fine facial hair, cold-reddened cheeks, chapped lips and visible breath vapor; true fabric weave on knit hats, nylon shells, fleece and denim; accurate contact shadows under boots, paws and hooves; natural depth with gentle phone-lens background falloff; clean edges with no oversharpening halos, no smearing, no waxy smoothing and no noise-reduction mush. Lighting is physically motivated by the real sky, sun and practical light sources only, with correct specular highlights on ice and wet surfaces. Targeting Seedance 2.5-level realism through Seedance 2.0.

11. REALISM AND CONTINUITY
Noah's moose-print thermal top and red flannel pants stay consistent; window glass pieces stay where they land; the snow mound stays in place; the room fills with visible cold fog from the open window. Physics must be correct: snow and ice crack, slide and fall with real weight, momentum and debris that scatters and settles; near-freezing water looks dark, heavy and slushy; nothing floats, morphs or reverses. Human anatomy is correct at all times — five fingers per hand, natural joint limits, believable balance, real slips, stumbles and recoveries. Animal behavior is species-accurate in gait, ear and tail signals, vocalizations and reactions; the animal never acts like a trained movie actor or a cartoon. Footprints, paw prints and disturbed snow persist once made. Breath vapor appears with exhales in the cold. Time of day, light direction and weather stay consistent for all 15 seconds.

12. AVOID
Plastic or waxy skin, airbrushed faces, doll eyes, animated or rubbery bodies, extra or fused fingers, warped limbs, morphing faces, changing outfits, shifting animal markings, CGI-looking snow, fake particle snow, glossy video-game ice, unnatural slow motion, cinematic music, title text, subtitles, watermarks, logos, lens dirt overlays, film grain filters, teal-orange grading, over-saturated colors, drone shots, impossible camera moves, perfect studio lighting, humans or animals floating, objects passing through each other, physics that reverses, professional rescue crews, uniforms, sirens, helicopters, staged hero poses, gore, blood or graphic injury. Also avoid: glass breaking in slow motion, snow pouring like liquid, the boy flying unrealistically far, the dog having glowing or human-like eyes.
```

## Prompt 003 — Shore Ice Shelf Calves Into Lake Minnewanka — Banff, Alberta

```text
1. FORMAT
Vertical 9:16 smartphone video, 1080x1920, 30fps, exactly 15.0 seconds, one continuous real-time take with no cuts, no slow motion, no speed ramps and no transitions. Native in-camera audio only — no music, no voice-over, no captions, no on-screen text, no logos, no watermark. It must read as a real clip filmed by an ordinary person in the moment and posted unedited.

2. LOOK
Bright blue-sky morning in February, -15°C, crisp mountain sunlight, strong reflections off polished ice, a light wind skimming spindrift across the surface, cold hard shadows from the pines. Raw social-media realism: the image has the slightly flat, true-to-life tonality of a phone camera in cold weather, with believable dynamic range, crisp but not oversharpened detail, faint rolling-shutter skew on the fastest pans, and realistic white-balance shifts as the lens moves between bright snow and darker subjects. Faces and hands look lived-in and cold, never airbrushed.

3. CAMERA
Tomas films handheld from the snowy shore, three meters behind Priya, framing her as she crouches at the edge of the ice ledge to photograph the bubbles; the ravens are visible on the snag frame left. Authentic handheld behavior throughout: micro-jitter from cold hands, breathing sway, footsteps transmitted into the frame, a brief autofocus hunt when the subject moves fast, exposure re-adjusting as the lens swings from bright snow to darker subjects, a 3–8 degree horizon tilt during the panic, and one fast whip-pan blur that resolves naturally. Standard phone main lens (about 26mm equivalent), no zoom pumping, no gimbal-smooth gliding, no drone-like motion.

4. LOCATION
The frozen south shore of Lake Minnewanka in Banff National Park, Alberta: a ledge of thick shore ice overhanging dark open water near a pressure crack, snow-draped limestone peaks across the lake, wind-polished green-black ice with trapped methane bubbles, scattered lodgepole pines on the bank.

5. CHARACTERS
Priya Raman, 29, South Asian, petite, long dark hair in a braid under a mustard-yellow beanie, black down jacket, grey snow pants, a small camera on a neck strap; her husband Tomas Lindqvist, 31, tall, blond stubble, navy shell jacket, red backpack, filming. A pair of common ravens perched on a broken lodgepole snag at the shoreline — glossy blue-black plumage, shaggy throat feathers, heavy curved beaks. Character consistency is locked: faces, body proportions, hairstyles, clothing colors, accessories and the animal's exact markings stay identical from the first frame to the last — no outfit changes, no face drift, no breed or coat changes.

6. HOOK (00:00–00:02)
00:00–00:01: Serene — Priya crouches on the shelf edge focusing her camera on frozen bubbles, the lake utterly silent. 00:01–00:02: Both ravens explode off the snag with harsh, rapid alarm croaks, wings thrashing right over her head, and a deep booming thunk travels through the ice under her boots.

7. TIMELINE
00:00–00:01 — Priya kneels at the lip of the ice shelf, camera to her eye, shooting the white methane bubble columns frozen in the green-black ice.
00:01–00:02 — The two ravens burst off the snag croaking loudly, flapping low over her; a deep thump resonates through the ice like a drum.
00:02–00:03 — Priya startles and looks up at the birds; behind her, a thin dark line races along the ice parallel to the shore.
00:03–00:04 — Tomas sees the line, shouts "Priya, back, back now!" The camera jerks forward as he steps onto the ice.
00:04–00:05 — The crack widens with a tearing screech; the whole ledge she kneels on drops a few centimeters toward the water.
00:05–00:06 — Priya scrambles backward on hands and knees, boots slipping on polished ice; the shelf tilts down at the lake end.
00:06–00:07 — Tomas lunges, grabs the shoulder strap of her down jacket with his free hand; the frame swings to show her terrified face.
00:07–00:08 — He yanks her across the widening crack; her knee drags over the gap as cold water spurts up through it.
00:08–00:09 — They both fall backward onto the snowy shore ice; the camera points skyward for a split second at the circling ravens.
```

```text
Laura films handheld from near the base, looking up the dune at Hannah sliding down and Noah waiting at the bottom, Maple running between them.
```
